Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Roadmap and proposed systems are development targets, not achieved claims. Only what has actually been built, tested, and verified is described as such.
  • JAH live retrieval was NOT verified.
  • Demo code runs locally for checking only and is NOT a hardened sandbox.
  • A SHA-256 fingerprint identifies a record; it does not prove ownership or consciousness.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established anywhere in this package.
  • Singularity and God-Level framing is a SIGNATURE DESIGN CLAIM only, never established fact.
  • "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here.
  • "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible.

This website is the subject website for the JAH Boundless Creation Generator Database database, public edition. It holds the full database: all 24 records, each with its description, math proof, science proof, history, construction workflow, generator, AI, demo, and sources.

The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Evidence: documented — Records the full official specification for The Boundless Creation Lifecycle as part of the 100 God-Level resources catalog, with attribution kept straight: the catalog is another AI's pasted words, source NOT confirmed, and is NOT Manon's own wording unless he says so.

Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

Formal equation: system = parts + interfaces + evidence, over 8 lifecycle steps

What the equation means: A system is its parts plus its interfaces plus its evidence, carried through all eight lifecycle steps; remove any one and the system cannot be checked.

Formal status: stated, not machine-checked

4. History

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: New Family Proposals

JAH-JBCG-000001 · New Family Proposals

History: The catalog arrived as pasted text on 2026-11-17, titled 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources for the JAH Network Master AI. The text reads as another AI's words, like the Foundation, College, PhD, NASA, DARPA, and Future catalogs before it; its source is NOT confirmed, and it is NOT Manon's own wording unless he says so. The cross-check of all 100 items against the live manifest found this item, number 100, to be a genuine gap: no live or staged database covered it, so it was picked for Wave 1 as one of only eight genuine BUILD items, with no padding. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the live db-signature-boundless-creativity-engine pair, with 34 records in each edition, and the live boundless-database-civilization-engine pair, with 16 records in each edition, which are subject databases about creativity and civilization engines, not a generator that expands the ecosystem with new resource families under the paste's section 6 and section 7 standard. It also links the Foundation program's staged meta-generator and universal-construction-generator pairs, package-only, the live universal-generator database, with 300 records, and the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, the construction engine of the paste's section 10.

Dataset: 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources catalog, item #100, JAH Boundless Creation Generator; cross-check BACKLOG.md, Wave 1 pick; sources retrieved 2026-11-17.

Skeptical analysis: Three limits stay attached to any reading of this record. First, this is a specification, not a deployed system: its implementation status is proposed and its maturity is E3, and calling it deployed would misrepresent it. Second, No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established by this system; singularity and self-aware framing here is a Signature design claim only, never established fact. Third, the sources cited here establish background practice; they do not prove that this specification has been built or tested at scale. "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here. "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible. Both distinctions stay with every record in this program, in plain words. Glossary of network terms used in this record, each defined in plain words on first use. A database is a named collection of records with a fixed schema, a search method, and an index file. A record is one full file of data inside a database, with a unique identifier. A generator is a program that produces complete deliverables on demand from a seed, rather than storing every output. A meta-generator is a generator that creates and tests new generators. An ecosystem is a connected network of databases, websites, generators, and artificial intelligences that share records and interfaces. The Master AI is the single coordinating artificial intelligence of the JAH Network, under which the seven catalog levels are divisions, not separate intelligences. Signature means made by the JAH Signature system, as opposed to harvested public material. A design-study is a proposed design, labeled as a proposal, never presented as an established fact or a built system. Maturity is a label for how developed a resource is, on the E1 to E7, T1 to T3, and U scale used across the network. Implementation status is the paste's section 9 distinction: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, stated plainly for every resource. The lifecycle is the paste's section 7 sequence: Discover, Read, Specify, Build, Connect, Test, Validate, Expand. A JCC code is a condensed pointer to a database, file, and record, never a replacement for the full data. Provenance is the recorded source and history of a record. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live.

History: The catalog arrived as pasted text on 2026-11-17, titled 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources for the JAH Network Master AI. The text reads as another AI's words, like the Foundation, College, PhD, NASA, DARPA, and Future catalogs before it; its source is NOT confirmed, and it is NOT Manon's own wording unless he says so. The cross-check of all 100 items against the live manifest found this item, number 100, to be a genuine gap: no live or staged database covered it, so it was picked for Wave 1 as one of only eight genuine BUILD items, with no padding. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the live db-signature-boundless-creativity-engine pair, with 34 records in each edition, and the live boundless-database-civilization-engine pair, with 16 records in each edition, which are subject databases about creativity and civilization engines, not a generator that expands the ecosystem with new resource families under the paste's section 6 and section 7 standard. It also links the Foundation program's staged meta-generator and universal-construction-generator pairs, package-only, the live universal-generator database, with 300 records, and the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, the construction engine of the paste's section 10.

Dataset: 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources catalog, item #100, JAH Boundless Creation Generator; cross-check BACKLOG.md, Wave 1 pick; sources retrieved 2026-11-17.

Skeptical analysis: Three limits stay attached to any reading of this record. First, this is a specification, not a deployed system: its implementation status is generated and its maturity is E2, and calling it deployed would misrepresent it. Second, No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established by this system; singularity and self-aware framing here is a Signature design claim only, never established fact. Third, the sources cited here establish background practice; they do not prove that this specification has been built or tested at scale. "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here. "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible. Both distinctions stay with every record in this program, in plain words. Glossary of network terms used in this record, each defined in plain words on first use. A database is a named collection of records with a fixed schema, a search method, and an index file. A record is one full file of data inside a database, with a unique identifier. A generator is a program that produces complete deliverables on demand from a seed, rather than storing every output. A meta-generator is a generator that creates and tests new generators. An ecosystem is a connected network of databases, websites, generators, and artificial intelligences that share records and interfaces. The Master AI is the single coordinating artificial intelligence of the JAH Network, under which the seven catalog levels are divisions, not separate intelligences. Signature means made by the JAH Signature system, as opposed to harvested public material. A design-study is a proposed design, labeled as a proposal, never presented as an established fact or a built system. Maturity is a label for how developed a resource is, on the E1 to E7, T1 to T3, and U scale used across the network. Implementation status is the paste's section 9 distinction: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, stated plainly for every resource. The lifecycle is the paste's section 7 sequence: Discover, Read, Specify, Build, Connect, Test, Validate, Expand. A JCC code is a condensed pointer to a database, file, and record, never a replacement for the full data. Provenance is the recorded source and history of a record. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live.

Dataset: 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources catalog, item #100, JAH Boundless Creation Generator; cross-check BACKLOG.md, Wave 1 pick; sources retrieved 2026-11-17.

Skeptical analysis: Three limits stay attached to any reading of this record. First, this is a specification, not a deployed system: its implementation status is generated and its maturity is E3, and calling it deployed would misrepresent it. Second, No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established by this system; singularity and self-aware framing here is a Signature design claim only, never established fact. Third, the sources cited here establish background practice; they do not prove that this specification has been built or tested at scale. "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here. "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible. Both distinctions stay with every record in this program, in plain words. Glossary of network terms used in this record, each defined in plain words on first use. A database is a named collection of records with a fixed schema, a search method, and an index file. A record is one full file of data inside a database, with a unique identifier. A generator is a program that produces complete deliverables on demand from a seed, rather than storing every output. A meta-generator is a generator that creates and tests new generators. An ecosystem is a connected network of databases, websites, generators, and artificial intelligences that share records and interfaces. The Master AI is the single coordinating artificial intelligence of the JAH Network, under which the seven catalog levels are divisions, not separate intelligences. Signature means made by the JAH Signature system, as opposed to harvested public material. A design-study is a proposed design, labeled as a proposal, never presented as an established fact or a built system. Maturity is a label for how developed a resource is, on the E1 to E7, T1 to T3, and U scale used across the network. Implementation status is the paste's section 9 distinction: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, stated plainly for every resource. The lifecycle is the paste's section 7 sequence: Discover, Read, Specify, Build, Connect, Test, Validate, Expand. A JCC code is a condensed pointer to a database, file, and record, never a replacement for the full data. Provenance is the recorded source and history of a record. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live.

5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: New Family Proposals

JAH-JBCG-000001 · New Family Proposals

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for New Family Proposals inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies New Family Proposals as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is inputs. Its inputs are the records, requirements, and prior decisions that arrive before this step begins, each named, dated, and sourced, so nothing enters this step unnamed or uncheckable. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for New Family Proposals that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach New Family Proposals inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the live db-signature-boundless-creativity-engine pair, with 34 records in each edition, and the live boundless-database-civilization-engine pair, with 16 records in each edition, which are subject databases about creativity and civilization engines, not a generator that expands the ecosystem with new resource families under the paste's section 6 and section 7 standard. It also links the Foundation program's staged meta-generator and universal-construction-generator pairs, package-only, the live universal-generator database, with 300 records, and the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, the construction engine of the paste's section 10.

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: Family Websites

JAH-JBCG-000005 · Family Websites

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Family Websites inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies Family Websites as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is handover. Its handover is a named handoff: the receiving step gets the specification, the evidence, and the open questions in writing, and the handover is only complete when the receiver can run the checks. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: Family Testing

JAH-JBCG-000009 · Family Testing

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Family Testing inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies Family Testing as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is checks. Its checks are run by execution, not by reading: the demo recomputes the record's figures twice and compares digests, the program reruns the same computation, and any mismatch stops the step. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: Family Retirement

JAH-JBCG-000013 · Family Retirement

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Family Retirement inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies Family Retirement as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is inputs. Its inputs are the records, requirements, and prior decisions that arrive before this step begins, each named, dated, and sourced, so nothing enters this step unnamed or uncheckable. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

JAH Boundless Creation Generator: Family Metrics

JAH-JBCG-000017 · Family Metrics

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Family Metrics inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies Family Metrics as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is handover. Its handover is a named handoff: the receiving step gets the specification, the evidence, and the open questions in writing, and the handover is only complete when the receiver can run the checks. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Family Metrics that a builder can follow and a checker can test. JAH Boundless Creation Generator: Family Expansion Beyond 700

JAH-JBCG-000021 · Family Expansion Beyond 700

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Family Expansion Beyond 700 inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record how the JAH ecosystem grows beyond its original catalog: how a new resource family is proposed, judged feasible, specified, built as a database, a website, a generator, and an artificial intelligence, integrated with the families that already exist, tested, launched, and grown. The paste's section 8 counts 700 planned resource types across seven levels, and its section 9 orders the catalog to expand beyond those 700 without an arbitrary limit, subject to available computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources. That expansion is not automatic and not free: each new family must earn its place by the same checks as every family before it. Every record here specifies one part of that expansion completely enough that a builder could implement it and a checker could test it.

Method: This record specifies Family Expansion Beyond 700 as one working part of the JAH Boundless Creation Generator. Its working angle is checks. Its checks are run by execution, not by reading: the demo recomputes the record's figures twice and compares digests, the program reruns the same computation, and any mismatch stops the step. Scope: The scope covers ecosystem expansion: new family proposals, family feasibility, family specifications, family databases, family websites, family generators, family artificial intelligences, family integration, family testing, family launch, family growth, family records, family retirement, cross-family links, family templates, family governance, family metrics, family documentation, family accessibility, family security, family expansion beyond 700, boundless catalog rules, the creation registry, and the boundless creation lifecycle. Outside the scope are the subject contents of the new families, which belong to their own databases once built, and any claim that expansion is unlimited, which it is not: expansion is bounded by computing, storage, data, permissions, and physical resources, as the paste itself states. This database records how expansion is done; it does not promise that anything can be created. Requirements: Every expansion method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must check the existing catalog before proposing a new family, so nothing is built twice. Second, it must judge feasibility with stated evidence, including what the new family needs and what it costs. Third, it must specify the new family completely: its database, its website, its generator, and its artificial intelligence, each with acceptance tests. Fourth, it must integrate the new family with the families that already exist, with links both ways. Fifth, it must test the new family by execution before launch. Sixth, it must record the new family in the creation registry with its identifier, its version, and its status. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: Ecosystem expansion connects through four interfaces. The catalog interface reads the existing registry before any proposal, so duplicates are caught at the start. The specification interface accepts a complete family specification: database, website, generator, and artificial intelligence, each with its tests. The build interface runs the family generators in order and writes each deliverable to the family record. The registry interface records the launched family with its identifier, its version, its links, and its status, so the Master AI can navigate to it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every expansion is stored with its proposal, its evidence, and its verdict, so the growth of the catalog can be audited family by family. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Family Expansion Beyond 700 that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E2. Implementation status: generated, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: generated, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach The Boundless Creation Lifecycle inside the JAH Boundless Creation Generator: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the live db-signature-boundless-creativity-engine pair, with 34 records in each edition, and the live boundless-database-civilization-engine pair, with 16 records in each edition, which are subject databases about creativity and civilization engines, not a generator that expands the ecosystem with new resource families under the paste's section 6 and section 7 standard. It also links the Foundation program's staged meta-generator and universal-construction-generator pairs, package-only, the live universal-generator database, with 300 records, and the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, the construction engine of the paste's section 10.

Verification path: Re-read the catalog item #100 and the cross-check row for it; confirm the slug and prefix are the pair's own; run this record's demo and confirm Deterministic: MATCH; confirm the maturity label and implementation status appear in the record; confirm the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions appear in plain words; confirm every cited source returns HTTP 200.

6. Generator

Generator: seeded boundless generator, prefix JAH-JBCG-GEN-, hard stop 1,000,000,000 outputs per file, throttled to 100 per call. Generator ceiling: up to 1,000,000,000 scales per file, generated on demand by the file AI — not stored files.

Each record carries the same boundless generator: it makes full records on demand; generated content is labeled simulated for weeding and double-checked.

7. AI

The AI of this database is JAH Boundless Creation Librarian. It explains this database in plain, conversational language, answers questions about any of the 24 records, and can create an AI assistant exactly like itself. It answers from this database's own data only, and says plainly when a question goes beyond it.

  • ai-thing.json — the per-database AI spec (the database's own AI, JAH Boundless Creation Librarian).
  • ai-discussion.json — the discussion AI spec, built to discuss JAH Boundless Creation Generator Database with a visitor, record by record.

Both are free downloads, free forever, no sign-in.

8. Demo

Every record in this database carries a working demo program and a boundless generator. The demo for this website is demo.js — run it with node demo.js.
